\title{Rule thirty: the two places}

\begin{section}{local-laws}
\heading{Local laws}
  \begin{lemma}{white-interior}
  \noaxioms
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), if \(a = false\), then if \(b = false\), then if \(c = false\), then \(rule(a, b, c) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(ha\), \(hb\), \(hc\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{ha}
  \forward{hb}
  \forward{hc}
  \end{rewrite}
  Close the goal by reflexivity.
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{white-run-interior}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and integer \(p\), if \(cell(t, leftof(p)) = false\), then if \(cell(t, p) = false\), then if \(cell(t, rightof(p)) = false\), then \(cell(succ(t), p) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hl\), \(hc\), \(hr\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{cellsucc(t, p)}
  \forward{hl}
  \forward{hc}
  \forward{hr}
  \end{rewrite}
  Close the goal by reflexivity.
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{black-run-interior}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and integer \(p\), if \(cell(t, leftof(p)) = true\), then if \(cell(t, p) = true\), then \(cell(succ(t), p) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hl\), \(hc\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{cellsucc(t, p)}
  \forward{hl}
  \forward{hc}
  \end{rewrite}
  Close the goal with \(ruleblackblack(cell(t, rightof(p)))\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{white-pair-right}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and integer \(p\), if \(cell(t, p) = false\), then if \(cell(t, rightof(p)) = false\), then \(cell(succ(t), p) = cell(t, leftof(p))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hc\), \(hr\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{cellsucc(t, p)}
  \forward{hc}
  \forward{hr}
  \forward{rulewhitewhite(cell(t, leftof(p)))}
  \end{rewrite}
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{white-pair-left}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and integer \(p\), if \(cell(t, leftof(p)) = false\), then if \(cell(t, p) = false\), then \(cell(succ(t), p) = cell(t, rightof(p))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hl\), \(hc\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{cellsucc(t, p)}
  \forward{hl}
  \forward{hc}
  \forward{ruleleftedge(cell(t, rightof(p)))}
  \end{rewrite}
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{theorem}{white-run-shrinks}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and integer \(a\) and integer \(b\), if for every integer \(q\), if \(a intle q\), then if \(q intle b\), then \(cell(t, q) = false\), then for every integer \(q\), if \(rightof(a) intle q\), then if \(q intle leftof(b)\), then \(cell(succ(t), q) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\), \(q\), \(h1\), \(h2\).
  \begin{apply}{\reference{Paper::white-run-interior}(t, q)}
  \begin{premise}{1}
  Close the goal with \(h(leftof(q), leleftof(h1), leofrightof(leftofle(leofrightof(rightofle(h2)))))\).
  \end{premise}
  \begin{premise}{2}
  Close the goal with \(h(q, leofrightof(h1), leofrightof(rightofle(h2)))\).
  \end{premise}
  \begin{premise}{3}
  Close the goal with \(h(rightof(q), lerightof(leofrightof(h1)), rightofle(h2))\).
  \end{premise}
  \end{apply}
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{diagonal-laws}
\heading{Diagonal laws}
  \begin{lemma}{reset}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and integer \(p\), if \(cell(t, p) = true\), then \(cell(succ(t), p) = not(cell(t, leftof(p)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hc\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{cellsucc(t, p)}
  \forward{hc}
  \end{rewrite}
  Close the goal with \(rulereset(cell(t, leftof(p)), cell(t, rightof(p)))\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{toggle}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and integer \(p\), if \(cell(t, p) = false\), then \(cell(succ(t), p) = bxor(cell(t, leftof(p)), cell(t, rightof(p)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hc\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{cellsucc(t, p)}
  \forward{hc}
  \end{rewrite}
  Close the goal with \(ruletoggle(cell(t, leftof(p)), cell(t, rightof(p)))\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{left-system}
\heading{The left system}
  \begin{lemma}{edge-black}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(t\), \(ldiag(0, t) = true\).
  \begin{proof}
  \begin{cases}{t}
  \begin{case}{lexlean.std.nat::zero}
  \bind{}
  Close the goal by reflexivity.
  \end{case}
  \begin{case}{lexlean.std.nat::succ}
  \bind{s}
  Close the goal with \(ldiagzero(s)\).
  \end{case}
  \end{cases}
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{diag-reset}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(s\), if \(ldiag(succ(k), s) = true\), then \(ldiag(succ(succ(k)), succ(s)) = not(ldiag(k, s))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{ldiagstep(k, s)}
  \forward{h}
  \end{rewrite}
  Close the goal with \(bxorreset(ldiag(k, s), ldiag(succ(succ(k)), s))\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{diag-toggle}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(s\), if \(ldiag(succ(k), s) = false\), then \(ldiag(succ(succ(k)), succ(s)) = bxor(ldiag(k, s), ldiag(succ(succ(k)), s))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  \begin{rewrite}{goal}
  \forward{ldiagstep(k, s)}
  \forward{h}
  \end{rewrite}
  Close the goal with \(bxortoggle(ldiag(k, s), ldiag(succ(succ(k)), s))\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{theorem}{leading-digits}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(j\), there exists a natural number \(P\) such that \(0 < P\) and there exists a natural number \(T\) such that for every natural number \(t\), if \(T ≤ t\), then \(ldiag(j, t + P) = ldiag(j, t)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(ldiagperiodic(j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{intent}
\heading{Intent}
  \begin{theorem}{gate-theorem}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(frontobligation\), then if \(zeroobligation\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hf\), \(hz\).
  Close the goal with \(gate(hf, hz)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{recompile-points}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(N\), there exists a natural number \(z\) such that \(N ≤ z\) and \(zerodiagonal(z)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(zerodiagonals(N)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{unbounded-periods}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then not \(commonperiod(P)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(nocommonperiod(P, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{compile-points}
\heading{Compile points}
  \begin{theorem}{black-after-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then \(blackdiagonal(z + 2)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(blackafterzero(z, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{zero-before-black}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(b\), if \(blackdiagonal(b + 2)\), then \(zerodiagonal(b)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(zerobeforeblack(b, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{isolated-zeros}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then not \(zerodiagonal(z + 1)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(zerothennonzero(z, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{doubling-points}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then there exists a natural number \(z\) such that \(zerodiagonal(z)\) and \(doublesat(P, z)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(doublingpoints(P, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{antiperiodic-doubling}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(P\) and natural number \(z\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then if \(doublesat(P, z)\), then \(antiperiodicdiagonal(P, z + 1)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hz\), \(hd\).
  Close the goal with \(antiperiodicafterdoubles(P, z, hz, hd)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{compile-bit}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  \(compilebitlaw\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(compilebit\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{right-system}
\heading{The right system}
  \begin{lemma}{right-law}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(s\), \(rdiag(succ(succ(k)), succ(s)) = bxor(rdiag(succ(succ(k)), s), rdiag(succ(k), s) bor rdiag(k, s))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(rdiagstep(k, s)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{theorem}{right-periods}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(j\) and natural number \(t\), \(rdiag(j, t + rpow(j)) = rdiag(j, t)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(rdiagperiodic(j, t)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{mask-form}
  \allowaxioms{propext}
  if \(maskobligation\), then for every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(readoutperiodic(T, P)\), then \(maskedperiodic(T, P)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.right::mask-unfold}(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{mask-sufficient}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(maskobligation\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hm\).
  Close the goal with \(problemoneofmaskpos(hm)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{mask-necessary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(problemone\), then \(maskobligation\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(maskposofproblemone(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{center-reproduces}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\), \(centercol(t + 1) = phicenter(t)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(centerfixed(t)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{fixed-sufficient}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(noperiodicfixed\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(problemoneofnpf(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{fixed-necessary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(problemone\), then \(noperiodicfixed\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(npfofproblemone(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{known-rules}
\heading{Projection onto known rules}
  \begin{theorem}{split-onefifty}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(rule(a, b, c) = bxor(ruleonefifty(a, b, c), b band c)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(splitonefifty(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{split-ninety}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(rule(a, b, c) = bxor(ruleninety(a, b, c), b band not(c))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(splitninety(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{split-sixty}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(rule(a, b, c) = bxor(rulesixty(a, b, c), not(b) band c)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(splitsixty(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{split-fifteen}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(rule(a, b, c) = bxor(rulefifteen(a, b, c), not(b) band not(c))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(splitfifteen(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{deep-hole}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(u0\) and truth value \(u1\) and truth value \(u2\) and truth value \(u3\), \(2 ≤ distthirty(u0, u1, u2, u3)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(deephole(u0, u1, u2, u3)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{center-response}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\), \(centercol(t) = not(respsixty(t))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(centerrespsixty(t)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{orthogonal-responses}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\), if \(1 ≤ t\), then \(bxor(respsixty(t), respfifteen(t)) = eventime(t)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(resprelation(t, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{projection-sixty-sufficient}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(respsixtyaperiodic\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(poneofrsixty(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{projection-sixty-necessary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(problemone\), then \(respsixtyaperiodic\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(rsixtyofpone(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{projection-fifteen-sufficient}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(respfifteenaperiodic\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(poneofrfifteen(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{projection-fifteen-necessary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(problemone\), then \(respfifteenaperiodic\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(rfifteenofpone(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{displacement-carry}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(displacement(a, b, c) = b band c\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(displacementiscarry(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{frame-direction}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(displacement(not(a), b, c) = displacement(a, b, c)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(framedirection(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{affine-left-linear}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(ruleonefifty(not(a), b, c) = not(ruleonefifty(a, b, c))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(affineleftlinear(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{irrational-sufficient}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(irrationalthirty\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(poneofirr(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{irrational-necessary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(problemone\), then \(irrationalthirty\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(irrofpone(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{frame-projection}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every truth value \(a\) and truth value \(b\) and truth value \(c\), \(iadd(sgn(rule(a, b, c)), sgn(rule(a, b, c))) = iadd(iadd(sgn(ruleonefifty(a, b, c)), sgn(ruleninety(a, b, c))), iadd(sgn(rulesixty(a, b, c)), sgn(rulefifteen(a, b, c))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(frameprojection(a, b, c)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{dyadic-scale}
\heading{The dyadic scale}
  \begin{lemma}{window-law}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(m\) and integer \(p\), \(window(t0, succ(m), p) = bxor(bxor(window(t0, m, leftof(p)), window(t0, m, p)), carry(t0 + m, p))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(windowstep(t0, m, p)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{lemma}{scale-doubles}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(j\), \(twopow(succ(j)) = twopow(j) + twopow(j)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(twopowsucc(j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{lemma}

  \begin{theorem}{scale-identity}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\), \(center(t + twopow(k)) = bxor(bxor(center(t), cell(t, negpow(k))), response(t, k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(scalecenter(t, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-scale}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(t\), if \(T ≤ t\), then \(bxor(cell(t, negpow(k)), response(t, k)) = bxor(center(t + lag(P, k)), center(t))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.scale::periodic-scale}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-scale-cone}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(t\), if \(T ≤ t\), then if \(t < twopow(k)\), then \(response(t, k) = bxor(center(t + lag(P, k)), center(t))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.scale::periodic-scale-cone}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{response-split}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\), \(response(t, k) = bxor(leftresponse(t, k), maskparity(t, k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(split(t, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-mask}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(t\), if \(T ≤ t\), then if \(t < twopow(k)\), then \(maskparity(t, k) = bxor(leftresponse(t, k), bxor(center(t + lag(P, k)), center(t)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.scale::periodic-mask}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{noperiod-sufficient}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(noperiod\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(poneofnoperiod(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{noperiod-necessary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(problemone\), then \(noperiod\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(noperiodofpone(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}
\end{section}

\begin{section}{witt-interfaces}
\heading{Witt forms and linear interfaces}
  \begin{theorem}{witt-split}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(y\), \(wittphi(y) = wittxor(wittlinear(y), wittshl(wittcarry(y), 1))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(phisplit(y)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{parity-interface}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(y\), \(rowpar(wittphi(y)) = bxor(rowpar(y), rowpar(wittcarry(y)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(parphi(y)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{cyclotomic-first-interface}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(y\), \(vala(wittphi(y)) = valb(wittcarry(y))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(valaphi(y)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{cyclotomic-second-interface}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(y\), \(valb(wittphi(y)) = bxor(vala(wittcarry(y)), valb(wittcarry(y)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(valbphi(y)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-row-rdiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(j\), \(wittbit(wittrow(t), j) = bton(rdiag(j, t))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(bitrowrdiag(t, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-center-rcenter}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\), \(wittcenter(t) = bton(rcenter(t))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(rowcenterrcenter(t)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-center-cell}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\), \(wittcenter(t) = bton(center(t))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(rowcentercenter(t)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-noperiod-sufficient}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(norowcenterperiod\), then \(problemone\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(poneofnorowcenterperiod(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-noperiod-necessary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  if \(problemone\), then \(norowcenterperiod\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(norowcenterperiodofpone(h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-freshman}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(y\), \(wittlinpow(twopow(k), y) = wittscale(k, y)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(wittfreshman(k, y)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-duhamel}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(n\), \(wittrow(t0 + n) = wittxor(wittlinpow(n, wittrow(t0)), wittwin(t0, n))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(wittduhamel(t0, n)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-scale-row}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), \(wittrow(t0 + twopow(k)) = wittxor(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0)), wittwin(t0, twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(wittrowscale(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-scale-cone}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\), if \(t < twopow(k)\), then \(center(t + twopow(k)) = bxor(center(t), wittresp(t, k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::witt-cone-scale}(t, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-cone-duality}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\), if \(t < twopow(k)\), then \(wittresp(t, k) = response(t, k)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::witt-cone-duality}(t, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-late-center}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(center(d) = bxor(wittresp(d + twopow(k), k), response(d + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::witt-late-center}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{two-places-witt}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(bxor(center(d), ldiag(d, d + twopow(k))) = bxor(bxor(center(d + twopow(k) + twopow(k)), center(d + twopow(k))), wittresp(d + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::two-places-witt}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{maskparity-coboundary}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), \(bxor(bxor(maskparity(d, succ(k)), maskparity(d, k)), maskparity(d + twopow(k), k)) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(maskparitycoboundary(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{ldiag-response-coboundary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(ldiag(d, d + twopow(k)) = bxor(bxor(response(d, succ(k)), response(d, k)), response(d + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::ldiag-response-coboundary}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{ldiag-leftresponse-coboundary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(ldiag(d, d + twopow(k)) = bxor(bxor(leftresponse(d, succ(k)), leftresponse(d, k)), leftresponse(d + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::ldiag-leftresponse-coboundary}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{two-places-witt-coboundary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(bxor(center(d), ldiag(d, d + twopow(k))) = bxor(bxor(wittresp(d, succ(k)), wittresp(d, k)), wittresp(d + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::two-places-witt-coboundary}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-scale}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(rdiag(d, s + twopow(k)) = rdiag(d, s)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-scale-eq}(d, s, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{two-places-settled}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(d, k)\), then \(damageeq(d, bxor(bxor(center(d + twopow(k) + twopow(k)), center(d + twopow(k))), wittresp(d + twopow(k), k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::two-places-settled}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{zcenter-leftresponse-coboundary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(d, k)\), then \(zcentereq(d, bxor(bxor(leftresponse(d, succ(k)), leftresponse(d, k)), leftresponse(d + twopow(k), k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::zcenter-leftresponse-coboundary}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{damage-witt-coboundary}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(d, k)\), then \(damageeq(d, bxor(bxor(wittresp(d, succ(k)), wittresp(d, k)), wittresp(d + twopow(k), k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::damage-witt-coboundary}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{zcenter-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then \(zcentereq(z, false)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hz\).
  Close the goal with \(zcenterofzero(z, hz)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{zcenter-after-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then \(zcentereq(z + 2, true)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hz\).
  Close the goal with \(zcenterafterzero(z, hz)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{zcenter-oscillates}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(N\), there exists a natural number \(z\) such that \(N ≤ z\) and \(zcentereq(z, false)\) and \(zcentereq(z + 2, true)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(zcenteroscillates(N)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{leftresponse-coboundary-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\) and natural number \(k\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then if \(z ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(z, k)\), then \(bxor(bxor(leftresponse(z, succ(k)), leftresponse(z, k)), leftresponse(z + twopow(k), k)) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::leftresponse-coboundary-zero}(z, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{leftresponse-coboundary-after-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\) and natural number \(k\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then if \(z + 2 ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(z + 2, k)\), then \(bxor(bxor(leftresponse(z + 2, succ(k)), leftresponse(z + 2, k)), leftresponse(z + 2 + twopow(k), k)) = true\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::leftresponse-coboundary-after-zero}(z, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-coboundary-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\) and natural number \(k\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then if \(z ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(z, k)\), then \(bxor(bxor(wittresp(z, succ(k)), wittresp(z, k)), wittresp(z + twopow(k), k)) = center(z)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::witt-coboundary-zero}(z, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-coboundary-after-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(z\) and natural number \(k\), if \(zerodiagonal(z)\), then if \(z + 2 ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(z + 2, k)\), then \(bxor(bxor(wittresp(z + 2, succ(k)), wittresp(z + 2, k)), wittresp(z + 2 + twopow(k), k)) = not(center(z + 2))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::witt-coboundary-after-zero}(z, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-response-lag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\) and natural number \(t\), if \(lag(P, k1) = lag(P, k2)\), then if \(T ≤ t\), then if \(t < twopow(k1)\), then if \(t < twopow(k2)\), then \(response(t, k1) = response(t, k2)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-response-lag}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-witt-lag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\) and natural number \(t\), if \(lag(P, k1) = lag(P, k2)\), then if \(T ≤ t\), then if \(t < twopow(k1)\), then if \(t < twopow(k2)\), then \(wittresp(t, k1) = wittresp(t, k2)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-witt-lag}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-left-mask-lag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\) and natural number \(t\), if \(lag(P, k1) = lag(P, k2)\), then if \(T ≤ t\), then if \(t < twopow(k1)\), then if \(t < twopow(k2)\), then \(bxor(leftresponse(t, k1), leftresponse(t, k2)) = bxor(maskparity(t, k1), maskparity(t, k2))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-left-mask-lag}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-late-response-lag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\), if \(lag(P, k1) = lag(P, k2)\), then if \(T ≤ d\), then if \(d ≤ k1\), then if \(d ≤ k2\), then if \(settledat(d, k1)\), then if \(settledat(d, k2)\), then \(response(d + twopow(k1), k1) = response(d + twopow(k2), k2)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-late-response-lag}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-late-witt-lag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\), if \(lag(P, k1) = lag(P, k2)\), then if \(T ≤ d\), then if \(d ≤ k1\), then if \(d ≤ k2\), then if \(settledat(d, k1)\), then if \(settledat(d, k2)\), then \(wittresp(d + twopow(k1), k1) = wittresp(d + twopow(k2), k2)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-late-witt-lag}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-late-left-mask-lag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\), if \(lag(P, k1) = lag(P, k2)\), then if \(T ≤ d\), then if \(d ≤ k1\), then if \(d ≤ k2\), then if \(settledat(d, k1)\), then if \(settledat(d, k2)\), then \(bxor(leftresponse(d + twopow(k1), k1), leftresponse(d + twopow(k2), k2)) = bxor(maskparity(d + twopow(k1), k1), maskparity(d + twopow(k2), k2))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-late-left-mask-lag}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-ray-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(j\), if \(j < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), j) = bton(bxor(rdiag(j, t0 + twopow(k)), rdiag(j, t0)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-ray0}(t0, k, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-low-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(j\), if \(j ≤ k\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), j) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-low}(t0, k, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-ray-one}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(i\), if \(i < twopow(k)\), then if \(2 * t0 < i + twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), i + twopow(k)) = bton(bxor(rdiag(i + twopow(k), t0 + twopow(k)), rdiag(i, t0)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-ray1}(t0, k, i)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-ray-two}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(m\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d + m < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(d + m, twopow(k)), d + 2 * m + twopow(succ(k))) = bton(bxor(ldiag(d, d + m), ldiag(d, d + m + twopow(k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-ray2}(d, m, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-archimedean}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(d, twopow(k)), d + twopow(succ(k))) = bton(bxor(center(d), ldiag(d, d + twopow(k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-archimedean}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-settled-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(m\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(d + m < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + m)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(d + m, twopow(k)), d + 2 * m + twopow(succ(k))) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-settled-zero}(d, m, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-degree}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(j\), if \(2 * (t0 + twopow(k)) < j\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), j) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-degree}(t0, k, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{right-threshold-exists}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(k\), there exists a natural number \(J\) such that \(k ≤ J\) and \(J + 2 ≤ twopow(succ(k))\) and \(rightthreshold(k, J)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(existsrightthreshold(k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-threshold-gap}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\) and natural number \(j\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(j ≤ J\), then if \(j < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), j) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-threshold-gap}(t0, k, J, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-threshold-lead}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), J + 1) = 1\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-threshold-lead}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{threshold-lt-center-black}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(twopow(k) ≤ t\), then if \(center(t) = true\), then \(J < t\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::threshold-lt-center-true}(t, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-cyclotomic-first}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), \(vala(wittwin(t0, twopow(k))) = vala(wittrow(t0 + twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(valawinwitt(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-cyclotomic-second}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), \(valb(wittwin(t0, twopow(k))) = valb(wittrow(t0 + twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(valbwinwitt(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-parity}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), \(rowpar(wittwin(t0, twopow(k))) = bxor(rowpar(wittrow(t0 + twopow(k))), rowpar(wittrow(t0)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(parwinwitt(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-carry-xor}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(a\) and natural number \(b\), \(wittcarry(wittxor(a, b)) = wittxor(wittxor(wittcarry(a), wittcarry(b)), crosscarry(a, b))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(carryxor(a, b)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{witt-phi-xor}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(a\) and natural number \(b\), \(wittphi(wittxor(a, b)) = wittxor(wittxor(wittphi(a), wittphi(b)), wittshl(crosscarry(a, b), 1))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(phixor(a, b)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-base-step}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), \(wittwin(t0 + 1, twopow(k)) = wittxor(wittlinear(wittwin(t0, twopow(k))), wittshl(wittxor(wittcarry(wittrow(t0 + twopow(k))), wittscale(k, wittcarry(wittrow(t0)))), 1))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(winwittbasestep(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-cone-step}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), if \(2 * t0 + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittwin(t0 + 1, twopow(k)) = wittxor(wittphi(wittwin(t0, twopow(k))), wittshl(crosscarry(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0)), wittwin(t0, twopow(k))), 1))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::winwitt-cone-step}(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-triality-outside}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(j\), if \(2 * t0 < twopow(k)\), then if \(3 * twopow(k) ≤ j\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), j) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-triality-outside}(t0, k, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-one-cone}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(r\), if \(2 * t0 < twopow(k)\), then if \(r < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), r + twopow(k)) = bton(bxor(rdiag(r + twopow(k), t0 + twopow(k)), rdiag(r, t0)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-one-cone}(t0, k, r)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-center-slot}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), if \(t0 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), t0 + twopow(k)) = bton(bxor(center(t0 + twopow(k)), center(t0)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-center-slot}(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-one-below-thresh}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\) and natural number \(r\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(r ≤ J\), then if \(r < twopow(k)\), then if \(2 * t0 < r + twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), r + twopow(k)) = bton(bxor(rdiag(r + twopow(k), t0 + twopow(k)), rdiag(r, t0 + twopow(k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-one-below-thresh}(t0, k, J, r)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-one-at-thresh}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then if \(2 * t0 < J + 1 + twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), J + 1 + twopow(k)) = bton(not(bxor(rdiag(J + 1 + twopow(k), t0 + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 1, t0 + twopow(k)))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-one-at-thresh}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{threshold-center-antiperiod}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(rdiag(J + 1, J + 1 + twopow(k)) = not(center(J + 1))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::threshold-center-antiperiod}(k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{threshold-center-witt-resp}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittresp(J + 1, k) = not(bxor(center(J + 1 + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 1, J + 1 + twopow(k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::threshold-center-witt-resp}(k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{phiscale-row-cone-step}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), if \(2 * t0 + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0 + 1)) = wittphi(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::phiscale-row-cone-step}(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-unperturbed-sector-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\) and natural number \(j\), if \(2 * t0 + 1 < twopow(k)\), then if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(2 * t0 < J\), then if \(j ≤ twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0 + 1, twopow(k)), j) = wittbit(wittphi(wittwin(t0, twopow(k))), j)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-unperturbed-sector-zero}(t0, k, J, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-unperturbed-sector-one-gap}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(r\), if \(2 * t0 + 1 < twopow(k)\), then if \(2 * t0 + 2 < r\), then if \(r ≤ twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0 + 1, twopow(k)), r + twopow(k)) = wittbit(wittphi(wittwin(t0, twopow(k))), r + twopow(k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-unperturbed-sector-one-gap}(t0, k, r)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-one-gap-ldiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(m\), if \(1 ≤ m\), then if \(2 * t0 + m < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), 2 * t0 + m + twopow(k)) = bton(ldiag(\lexeme{lexlean.std.nat::sub}(twopow(k), m), t0 + twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-one-gap-ldiag}(t0, k, m)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-one-cone-edge}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), if \(2 * t0 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), 2 * t0 + twopow(k)) = bton(not(ldiag(twopow(k), t0 + twopow(k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-one-cone-edge}(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{ldiag-eq-rdiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\), \(ldiag(d, d + s) = rdiag(d + 2 * s, d + s)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(ldiageqrdiag(d, s)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-sub-ldiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d + 2 * s ≤ J\), then if \(d + 2 * s < twopow(k)\), then \(rdiag(d + 2 * s, d + s + twopow(k)) = ldiag(d, d + s)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-sub-ldiag}(d, s, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-succ-not-ldiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d + 2 * s = J + 1\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(rdiag(J + 1, d + s + twopow(k)) = not(ldiag(d, d + s))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-succ-not-ldiag}(d, s, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-one-ldiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d + 2 * s < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(d + s, twopow(k)), d + 2 * s + twopow(k)) = bton(bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + s)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-one-ldiag}(d, s, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-top-edge-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), if \(t0 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), 2 * t0 + twopow(succ(k))) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-top-edge-zero}(t0, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-second-edge-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(m\) and natural number \(k\), if \(1 + m < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(1 + m, twopow(k)), 1 + 2 * m + twopow(succ(k))) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-second-edge-zero}(m, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-late-false-window}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\) and natural number \(j\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(2 * t0 < j\), then if \(j ≤ J\), then \(rdiag(j, t0 + twopow(k)) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-late-false-window}(t0, k, J, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-late-true-plateau}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(2 * t0 ≤ J\), then \(rdiag(J + 1, t0 + twopow(k)) = true\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-late-true-plateau}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-late-false-odd-hit}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(2 * t0 = J + 1\), then \(rdiag(J + 1, t0 + twopow(k)) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-late-false-odd-hit}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-late-false-even-hit}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(2 * t0 = J\), then \(rdiag(J + 1, t0 + 1 + twopow(k)) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-late-false-even-hit}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-plus-two-step-neg}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(2 * t0 ≤ J\), then \(rdiag(J + 2, t0 + 1 + twopow(k)) = not(rdiag(J + 2, t0 + twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-plus-two-step-neg}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-thresh-plus-two-freeze}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(2 * t0 = J\), then if \(J + 2 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(t0 + 1, twopow(k)), J + 2) = wittbit(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), J + 2)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-thresh-plus-two-freeze}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{ldiag-two-false}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(m\), \(ldiag(2, 2 + m) = false\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(ldiagtwofalse(m)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-third-edge-zero}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(m\) and natural number \(k\), if \(2 + m < twopow(k)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(2 + m, twopow(k)), 2 + 2 * m + twopow(succ(k))) = 0\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-third-edge-zero}(m, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{ldiag-three-step-neg}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(m\), \(ldiag(3, 3 + m) = not(ldiag(3, 2 + m))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(ldiagthreestepneg(m)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-one-settled}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 * s < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s)\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(d + s, twopow(k)), d + 2 * s + twopow(k)) = bton(bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + s + twopow(k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-one-settled}(d, s, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-eq-ldiag-settled-below-thresh}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 * s ≤ J\), then if \(d + 2 * s < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s)\), then \(rdiag(d + 2 * s, d + s + twopow(k)) = ldiag(d, d + s + twopow(k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-eq-ldiag-settled-below-thresh}(d, s, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-not-ldiag-settled-at-thresh}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 * s = J + 1\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s)\), then \(rdiag(J + 1, d + s + twopow(k)) = not(ldiag(d, d + s + twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-not-ldiag-settled-at-thresh}(d, s, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-sector-two-scale-eq}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\), if \(d ≤ k1\), then if \(d ≤ k2\), then if \(d + s < twopow(k1)\), then if \(d + s < twopow(k2)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s + twopow(k1))\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s + twopow(k2))\), then \(wittbit(wittwin(d + s, twopow(k1)), d + 2 * s + twopow(succ(k1))) = wittbit(wittwin(d + s, twopow(k2)), d + 2 * s + twopow(succ(k2)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::bit-winwitt-sector-two-scale-eq}(d, s, k1, k2)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{center-late-xor-ldiag-witt}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then \(bxor(center(d + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + twopow(k))) = bxor(wittresp(d, succ(k)), wittresp(d + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::center-late-xor-ldiag-witt}(d, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-ldiag-xor-late-witt-lag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k1\) and natural number \(k2\), if \(lag(P, k1) = lag(P, k2)\), then if \(T ≤ d\), then if \(d ≤ k1\), then if \(d ≤ k2\), then \(bxor(ldiag(d, d + twopow(k1)), ldiag(d, d + twopow(k2))) = bxor(wittresp(d + twopow(k1), k1), wittresp(d + twopow(k2), k2))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-ldiag-xor-late-witt-lag}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-response-shift-p}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ t\), then if \(t + P < twopow(k)\), then \(response(t + P, k) = response(t, k)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-response-shift-p}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-witt-shift-p}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ t\), then if \(t + P < twopow(k)\), then \(wittresp(t + P, k) = wittresp(t, k)\).

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-below-threshold-eq-center}

  For every natural number \(r\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(r ≤ J\), then if \(r < twopow(k)\), then \(rdiag(r, r + twopow(k)) = center(r)\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-below-threshold-eq-center}(r, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-two-halves-below-threshold}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(r\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(T ≤ r\), then if \(r ≤ J\), then if \(r < twopow(k)\), then \(bxor(center(r), ldiag(r, r + twopow(k))) = bxor(bxor(center(r + lag(P, succ(k))), center(r + lag(P, k))), wittresp(r + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-two-halves-below-threshold}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-center-xor-witt-below-threshold}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(r\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(T ≤ r\), then if \(r ≤ J\), then if \(r < twopow(k)\), then \(bxor(center(r), wittresp(r + twopow(k), k)) = bxor(bxor(bxor(leftresponse(r, succ(k)), leftresponse(r, k)), leftresponse(r + twopow(k), k)), bxor(center(r + lag(P, succ(k))), center(r + lag(P, k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-center-xor-witt-below-threshold}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-two-halves-at-threshold}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(T ≤ J + 1\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(bxor(not(center(J + 1)), ldiag(J + 1, J + 1 + twopow(k))) = bxor(bxor(center(J + 1 + lag(P, succ(k))), center(J + 1 + lag(P, k))), wittresp(J + 1 + twopow(k), k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-two-halves-at-threshold}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-not-center-xor-witt-at-threshold}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(T ≤ J + 1\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(bxor(not(center(J + 1)), wittresp(J + 1 + twopow(k), k)) = bxor(bxor(bxor(leftresponse(J + 1, succ(k)), leftresponse(J + 1, k)), leftresponse(J + 1 + twopow(k), k)), bxor(center(J + 1 + lag(P, succ(k))), center(J + 1 + lag(P, k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Assume \(hP\), \(h\).
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::periodic-not-center-xor-witt-at-threshold}(T, P, hP, h)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{threshold-center-left-mask-eq-not-rdiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(bxor(leftresponse(J + 1, k), maskparity(J + 1, k)) = not(bxor(center(J + 1 + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 1, J + 1 + twopow(k))))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::threshold-center-left-mask-eq-not-rdiag}(k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{sector-one-below-threshold-eq-left-settled}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 * s ≤ J\), then if \(d + 2 * s < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s)\), then \(bxor(rdiag(d + 2 * s + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k)), rdiag(d + 2 * s, d + s + twopow(k))) = bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + s + twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::sector-one-below-threshold-eq-left-settled}(d, s, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{sector-one-at-threshold-eq-left-settled}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 * s = J + 1\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s)\), then \(not(bxor(rdiag(J + 1 + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 1, d + s + twopow(k)))) = bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + s + twopow(k)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::sector-one-at-threshold-eq-left-settled}(d, s, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-defect-step-below-threshold}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\) and natural number \(j\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(j ≤ J\), then \(bxor(bxor(rdiag(j + 2, t + 1 + twopow(k)), rdiag(j + 2, t + 1)), bxor(rdiag(j + 2, t + twopow(k)), rdiag(j + 2, t))) = bxor(rdiag(j + 1, t + twopow(k)), rdiag(j + 1, t)) band not(rdiag(j, t))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-defect-step-below-threshold}(t, k, J, j)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-plus-two-defect-step}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then \(bxor(bxor(rdiag(J + 2, t + 1 + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, t + 1)), bxor(rdiag(J + 2, t + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, t))) = not(rdiag(J, t))\).

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-plus-two-defect-step-ldiag}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d + 2 * s = J\), then \(bxor(bxor(rdiag(J + 2, d + s + 1 + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, d + s + 1)), bxor(rdiag(J + 2, d + s + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, d + s))) = not(ldiag(d, d + s))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-plus-two-defect-step-ldiag}(d, s, k, J)\).

  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-plus-two-center-decomp}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then \(rdiag(J + 2, J + 2 + twopow(k)) = bxor(bxor(center(J + 2), bxor(rdiag(J + 2, J + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, J))), bxor(center(J), rdiag(J, J + 1)))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-plus-two-center-decomp}(k, J)\).
  \end{proof}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-two-halves-at-threshold-plus-two}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(T ≤ J + 2\), then if \(J + 2 < twopow(k)\), then \(bxor(bxor(bxor(center(J + 2), bxor(rdiag(J + 2, J + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, J))), bxor(center(J), rdiag(J, J + 1))), ldiag(J + 2, J + 2 + twopow(k))) = bxor(bxor(center(J + 2 + lag(P, succ(k))), center(J + 2 + lag(P, k))), wittresp(J + 2 + twopow(k), k))\).

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-center-xor-witt-at-threshold-plus-two}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}

  \begin{theorem}{ldiag-damage-step-of-settled}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(settledby(d, s)\), then \(bxor(ldiag(d + 2, s + 1 + twopow(k)), ldiag(d + 2, s + 1)) = bxor(ldiag(d + 1, s + twopow(k)) bor ldiag(d + 2, s + twopow(k)), ldiag(d + 1, s) bor ldiag(d + 2, s))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::ldiag-damage-step-of-settled}(d, s, k)\).

  \begin{theorem}{ldiag-damage-step-of-settled-pair}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d + 1 ≤ k\), then if \(settledby(d, s)\), then if \(settledby(d + 1, s)\), then \(bxor(ldiag(d + 2, s + 1 + twopow(k)), ldiag(d + 2, s + 1)) = bxor(ldiag(d + 2, s + twopow(k)), ldiag(d + 2, s)) band not(ldiag(d + 1, s))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::ldiag-damage-step-of-settled-pair}(d, s, k)\).
  \end{proof}
  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{two-halves-off-center-witt-scale}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\), if \(d + 2 * s < twopow(k)\), then \(bitwinwitteq(d + s + twopow(k), twopow(k), d + 2 * s + twopow(succ(k)), bxor(bxor(rdiag(d + 2 * s, d + s + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + s + twopow(k))), bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(succ(k)), d + s + twopow(succ(k))), ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k)))))\).

  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(s\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(d ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 * s ≤ J\), then if \(d + 2 * s < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + s)\), then \(bitwinwitteq(d + s + twopow(k), twopow(k), d + 2 * s + twopow(succ(k)), bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(succ(k)), d + s + twopow(succ(k))), ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + s + twopow(k))))\).

  \begin{theorem}{colm1-dyadic-jump-eq-maskparity-zero-jump}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), \(bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(succ(k)), d + 1 + twopow(succ(k))), ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + 1 + twopow(k))) = bxor(bxor(maskparity(d + 1 + twopow(succ(k)), 0), maskparity(d + 1 + twopow(k), 0)), bxor(bxor(center(d + 1 + twopow(succ(k)) + 1), center(d + 1 + twopow(succ(k)))), bxor(center(d + 1 + twopow(k) + 1), center(d + 1 + twopow(k)))))\).

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-colm1-dyadic-jump-eq-leftresponse-diff}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ d + 1\), then if \(d + 2 ≤ k\), then \(bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(succ(k)), d + 1 + twopow(succ(k))), ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + 1 + twopow(k))) = bxor(bxor(leftresponse(d + 2, succ(k)), leftresponse(d + 2, k)), bxor(leftresponse(d + 1, succ(k)), leftresponse(d + 1, k)))\).

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-off-center-witt-below-threshold-eq-leftresponse}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(T ≤ d + 1\), then if \(d + 2 ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 ≤ J\), then if \(settledby(d, d + 1)\), then \(bitwinwitteq(d + 1 + twopow(k), twopow(k), d + 2 + twopow(succ(k)), bxor(bxor(leftresponse(d + 2, succ(k)), leftresponse(d + 2, k)), bxor(leftresponse(d + 1, succ(k)), leftresponse(d + 1, k))))\).

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-off-center-witt-at-threshold-eq-not-leftresponse}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then if \(T ≤ d + 1\), then if \(d + 2 ≤ k\), then if \(d + 2 = J + 1\), then if \(J + 1 < twopow(k)\), then if \(settledby(d, d + 1)\), then \(bitwinwitteq(d + 1 + twopow(k), twopow(k), J + 1 + twopow(succ(k)), not(bxor(bxor(leftresponse(d + 2, succ(k)), leftresponse(d + 2, k)), bxor(leftresponse(d + 1, succ(k)), leftresponse(d + 1, k)))))\).

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-plus-two-defect-cone}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(2 * t0 ≤ J + 1\), then \(bxor(rdiag(J + 2, t0 + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, t0)) = rdiag(J + 2, t0 + twopow(k))\).
  \begin{proof}
  Close the goal with \(\lexeme{r30.witt::rdiag-threshold-plus-two-defect-cone}(t0, k, J)\).
  \end{proof}

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-threshold-plus-two-defect-two-step}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(J\), if \(rightthreshold(k, J)\), then \(bxor(bxor(rdiag(J + 2, t + 2 + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, t + 2)), bxor(rdiag(J + 2, t + twopow(k)), rdiag(J + 2, t))) = bxor(rdiag(J, t), rdiag(J, t + 1))\).

  \end{theorem}

  \begin{theorem}{carry-row-scale-decomp}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), \(wittcarry(wittrow(t0 + twopow(k))) = wittxor(wittxor(wittcarry(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0))), wittcarry(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)))), crosscarry(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0)), wittwin(t0, twopow(k))))\).

  \begin{theorem}{carry-row-scale-cone}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), if \(2 * t0 + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(wittcarry(wittrow(t0 + twopow(k))) = wittxor(wittxor(wittscale(k, wittcarry(wittrow(t0))), wittcarry(wittwin(t0, twopow(k)))), crosscarry(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0)), wittwin(t0, twopow(k))))\).

  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), \(wittcarry(wittwin(t0 + twopow(k), twopow(k))) = wittxor(wittxor(wittcarry(wittwin(t0, twopow(succ(k)))), wittcarry(wittscale(k, wittwin(t0, twopow(k))))), crosscarry(wittwin(t0, twopow(succ(k))), wittscale(k, wittwin(t0, twopow(k)))))\).

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-crosscarry-center-slot-and}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(t0\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ t0\), then if \(t0 + 1 < twopow(k)\), then \(bitcrosscarryeq(wittscale(k, wittrow(t0)), wittwin(t0, twopow(k)), t0 + 1 + twopow(k), bxor(rdiag(t0 + 1, t0) band bxor(center(t0 + lag(P, k)), center(t0)), bxor(rdiag(t0 + 1 + twopow(k), t0 + twopow(k)), rdiag(t0 + 1, t0)) band center(t0)))\).

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-crosscarry-colm1-and}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ d + 1\), then if \(d + 2 < twopow(k)\), then \(bitcrosscarryeq(wittscale(k, wittrow(d + 1)), wittwin(d + 1, twopow(k)), d + 2 + twopow(k), bxor(ldiag(d, d + 1) band bxor(center(d + 1 + lag(P, k)), center(d + 1)), bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + 1 + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + 1)) band center(d + 1)))\).

  \begin{theorem}{crosscarry-row-scale-at-threshold-eq-rdiag}

  \begin{theorem}{winwitt-testbit-outside-cone}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-crosscarry-late-center-bit-shift-mul-at-white-centers}

  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(m\) and natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ d + 1\), then if \(d + 1 ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(d + 1, k)\), then if \(center(d + 1) = false\), then if \(center(d + 1 + lag(P, k)) = false\), then if \(center(d + 1 + lag(P, succ(k))) = false\), then \(zcenterandeq(d + 1, maskparity(d + 1 + twopow(succ(k)), 0), bxor(bxor(wittbitbool(crosscarry(wittscale(k, wittrow(d + m * P + 1 + twopow(k))), wittwin(d + m * P + 1 + twopow(k), twopow(k))), d + m * P + 1 + twopow(k) + twopow(k)), wittbitbool(crosscarry(wittscale(k, wittrow(d + 1 + twopow(k))), wittwin(d + 1 + twopow(k), twopow(k))), d + 1 + twopow(k) + twopow(k))), wittresp(d + m * P + 1 + twopow(k), k) band maskparity(d + m * P + 1 + twopow(succ(k)), 0)))\).

  \begin{theorem}{rdiag-restart}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}

  \begin{theorem}{periodic-winwitt-step-late-center-slot-of-zcenter-true}
  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ d + 1\), then if \(d + 1 ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(d + 1, k)\), then if \(zcentereq(d + 1, true)\), then if \(center(d + 1) = false\), then if \(center(d + 1 + lag(P, k)) = false\), then if \(center(d + 1 + lag(P, succ(k))) = false\), then \(bxor(maskparity(d + 1 + twopow(succ(k)), 0), bxor(maskparity(d + 1 + twopow(k), 0), maskparity(d + 1, 0))) = bxor(wittresp(d + 2 + twopow(k), k), wittbitbool(wittphi(wittwin(d + 1 + twopow(k), twopow(k))), d + 2 + twopow(k) + twopow(k)))\).

  \allowaxioms{Classical.choice;Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(T\) and natural number \(P\), if \(0 < P\), then if \(centerperiodic(T, P)\), then for every natural number \(d\) and natural number \(k\), if \(T ≤ d + 1\), then if \(d + 2 ≤ k\), then if \(settledat(d + 1, k)\), then if \(zcentereq(d + 1, true)\), then if \(center(d + 1) = false\), then if \(center(d + 1 + lag(P, k)) = false\), then if \(center(d + 1 + lag(P, succ(k))) = false\), then \(bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(succ(k)), d + 1 + twopow(succ(k))), bxor(ldiag(d + twopow(k), d + 1 + twopow(k)), ldiag(d, d + 1))) = bxor(wittbitbool(wittwin(d + 2 + twopow(k), twopow(k)), d + 3 + twopow(k) + twopow(k)), wittbitbool(wittphi(wittwin(d + 1 + twopow(k), twopow(k))), d + 3 + twopow(k) + twopow(k)))\).

  \begin{theorem}{rowagree-mono}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(Q\) and natural number \(a\) and natural number \(L\) and natural number \(a1\) and natural number \(L1\), if \(a ≤ a1\), then if \(a1 + L1 ≤ a + L\), then if \(rowagree(t, Q, a, L)\), then \(rowagree(t, Q, a1, L1)\).

  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(Q\) and natural number \(a\) and natural number \(L\), if \(rowagree(t, Q, a, L + 2)\), then \(rowagree(t + 1, Q, a + 2, L)\).

  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(Q\) and natural number \(a\) and natural number \(L\) and natural number \(s\), if \(rowagree(t, Q, a, L + 2 * s)\), then \(rowagree(t + s, Q, a + 2 * s, L)\).

  \begin{theorem}{phiscale-row-testbit-eq-of-rowagree}
  \allowaxioms{Quot.sound;propext}
  For every natural number \(t\) and natural number \(k\) and natural number \(Q\) and natural number \(a\) and natural number \(L\) and natural number \(i\), if \(twopow(succ(k)) ≤ a\), then if \(i ≤ L\), then if \(rowagree(t, Q, a - twopow(succ(k)), L)\), then if \(rowagree(t, Q, a - twopow(k), L)\), then if \(rowagree(t, Q, a, L)\), then \(wittbitbool(wittscale(k, wittrow(t + Q)), a + i + Q) = wittbitbool(wittscale(k, wittrow(t)), a + i)\).
  \begin{proof}

  \end{proof}
